The count is written now, and the proof is the game's own file: a 34-directory mount list the game wrote itself rebuilds through this module byte for byte, and taking the overlay out again reproduces the shipped file exactly.
- **New Item Studio: removing the overlay puts the texture registry back, to the copy from before the overlay.** `meta/0.pathc` is a loose file beside the archives rather than an entry inside one, so an overlay install has nowhere to write it except in place. Taking the overlay away left it rewritten, naming textures that went with the directory: a registry describing files the game no longer has. The install now keeps a copy of every loose file it is about to rewrite, outside the game folder and taken the first time the overlay touched it, and the removal puts those back and says which came back. Not the oldest backup the workbench holds: that is only as old as the first time this workbench touched the file, which may already have been a modified one, and restoring from it wrote a stale registry over a freshly verified install.
- **New Item Studio: removing the overlay puts the texture registry back.** `meta/0.pathc` is a loose file beside the archives rather than an entry inside one, so an overlay install has nowhere to put it except in place. Taking the overlay away left it rewritten, naming textures that went with the directory: a registry describing files the game no longer has. The removal now restores every loose file the workbench had rewritten, to the oldest copy its own backups hold -- the same rule the move into the overlay uses for the archives themselves -- and says which files came back.
- **Archive overlays: the directory block is a tree, which is the second half of why the game would not start.** Our writer put each folder's whole path in one record under the root, where every shipped archive writes `aiscript` and then `/bin__` hanging off it. Both resolve to the same string when a reader walks the parents -- this repository's reader does, which is why the overlay read back perfectly -- but the flat form tells the game the archive holds a root-level folder with slashes in its name rather than a tree it can walk down. With that and the missing intermediate folders both fixed, a shipped archive's own files back through the writer now come out **byte for byte identical**, header checksum included, on the two archives whose file names share no prefixes (0013 and 0016); the larger ones still differ only in how their file-name block factors common prefixes, which no reader depends on.
- **New Item Studio: an imported model no longer inherits the template's cloth and collision.** The plan copied every file of the template's family under the new stem, the mesh physics among them. That file binds cloth and collision to the *template's* own vertices, so on a model of your own the indices land wherever they land: an imported hammer came out with a handle that swings like a cape. The game finds the file by the stem, so an item written without one simply has none, which is now the default; the Model step has **Give the imported model the template's cloth and physics** for a template whose cloth you want and whose shape yours follows. A template-model item keeps its physics as before.
- **Archive overlays: the folder table carries every folder on the way down, which is what made the game refuse to start.** An overlay wrote a folder record only for the folders that held files, so `character/bin__/prefab/1_pc/01_phm/weapon/01_onehandweapon/x.prefab` put one record in the table where the shipped archives put seven. This repository's own reader was happy with that -- it resolves a file by its own record -- and the game was not: it mounted the directory, rewrote the mount list, and then stopped with "There may be a problem with the game installation. Please reinstall." Every shipped archive carries the whole tree: 123 of archive 0000's 1,370 folder records hold no file of their own. The writer now emits every ancestor, sorted by path, with the file ranges tiling the table as before. The gate is a parity check against the shipped archives themselves: a real archive's own files back through the writer come out with the same folder paths, hashes and ranges, and the same file names, order, sizes and flags (`-m real_game`, three archives; 0013 comes back byte for byte).
- **New Item Studio: the effect dialog says when the preview's shape is a stand-in.** A third of the shipped emitters spawn their particles on the surface of a mesh, and the archives do not carry every mesh they name. The preview scatters them around the anchor instead, which drew a compact cloud on a hammer's head while the game drew a metre of fire along the weapon. The reading was in the preview's notes, folded away under *What the effect is made of*; it is now a line of its own beside the viewport, naming the mesh and saying that the anchor is where the effect starts either way.
- **New Item Studio: an overlay install of an item with a texture of its own no longer fails, and step 7 says what each button did.** `install_overlay` asked a `MetaFileWrite` for `.payload`, which it does not have, so every overlay install of a plan carrying a rewritten `meta/0.pathc` -- that is, every item with a new `.dds` -- ended in an AttributeError at write time. The texture registry now goes into the overlay directory the way it goes into a patching install, backup and all. The four buttons on step 7 also all report through one signal and hand back four different kinds of result, and three of them have no `changed_paths` between them, so an overlay install, a move into the overlay and a removal each said "Installed 0 archive entr(ies)", which reads like a failure after a button that did its work; each route now says what it actually did. The overlay's `.paz` is padded out to the sixteen-byte boundary as well, which every shipped one is.
- **New Item Studio: the effect's sprites draw as sprites, and the gizmo is the size of the item.** Half the shipped effect sprites are BC7 in a DX10 header, a format the helper's CPU decoder has no case for, so those emitters drew as flat orange quads: fire that looked like a wall of paint. Sprites now upload their compressed blocks straight to Direct3D, which has understood BC7 since feature level 11, and are sampled as sRGB, which is how the sheets are authored -- read as linear, the dark surround of every sprite lifts to a grey the additive pass adds on top, and the fire arrives as a grid of rectangles with flames inside them. The fire sweep went from one texture to three. Separately, the placement package framed on everything it held, and one of those things is the effect's reach cage: a boss effect reaching twenty metres made the viewport's scene extent twenty metres, so the camera opened on an empty box with the item a speck in it, the ground grid drew squares two metres wide, and the placement gizmo -- whose arms are a fifth of the scene -- put its handles four metres past the edges of the view, where nothing could be grabbed. The package now declares the item and the character as its framing bounds, so the view opens on them and the gizmo is a third of a metre across. The anchor is sized from the item as well, a twenty-fifth of its longest side rather than a fixed centimetre and a half.
- **New Item Studio: a read that fails because the archives moved says so.** Another program rewriting the game's archives -- a mod manager mounting or unmounting them, a game update -- moves every payload, and the entries a snapshot was built from then point at the wrong bytes. What surfaced was the deepest error the read reached, "ChaCha20 decryption validation failed for ...", which says nothing about the cause. The snapshot now checks the archive's own index when a read fails and, when it disagrees, says where the file is now, where the workbench looked, and that the archives need reading again.
- **New Item Studio: "Sell for one copper" reaches the price the shop charges.** A `_statList_DataDefinedStaticLevel` record is five bytes, not the twenty-five the row reader assumed, and the twenty-byte slip put the cursor exactly one price entry into the level's own `_buyPriceList`: the tail of the first entry reads as a count of one and the second entry parses behind it, so a level that carried copper and camp money came back carrying only camp money. The copper price -- the one Tranan charges in -- was invisible to the Stats step, so one copper set the camp currency and left copper at the template's 1,170, and the item cost 60 silver in game (seen 2026-08-19). The Stats step now shows both price columns, and every one of the 6,578 shipped rows still rebuilds byte for byte.
- **New Item Studio: the weapon effect is placed on the model you imported, and the studio's own work no longer runs on the UI thread.** *Place in viewport* used the imported model only once *Apply the placement* had run, so an effect was usually judged against the template's blade; it now takes the imported model at the placement set on step 3 and says which mesh it is showing. The studio's worker signals were connected to closures, which Qt runs on the emitting thread, so every panel update after a snapshot, plan, export or install ran on the worker thread instead of the UI thread; they are bound methods now and come back queued.
- **New Item Studio: the preview no longer waits for a build that never starts.** The worker's `finished` handler was a plain function, so Qt ran it on the worker's thread and the follow-up build was scheduled on an event loop that was already quitting: a preview requested while another was building (importing a model right after choosing a template, every time) simply never started, and the viewport kept showing the scene before it. It is a bound method now, and a superseded build is stopped rather than waited out, so an import lands in about twelve seconds instead of waiting the template preview out first.
- **New Item Studio: the imported model shows, and the gizmo never lands on the template.** Three faults behind "the preview is bugged": a `ready` from the package *before* the newest request was taken as this scene's, so while the placement package was still building the studio put the gizmo, the placement and a camera fit on the template that was still on screen (the frame now remembers which package is loaded, and a ready for anything else is ignored; a new request also takes the gizmo off the stale scene at once and disables the placement controls until the model is there); the model's *raw* bounds were handed to the viewport instead of the baked ones, so every placement push rewrote the editable role's world bounds to a five-centimetre box and the camera framed that; and, from this session's own work in progress, the finished build was read back through a lambda on the worker's `completed` signal, which Qt runs on the worker thread, so the viewport's process was created off the UI thread and never reported ready. The build in flight is named on the frame and read back by a bound method; a test guards it. While a preview builds, the status says so and the placement controls are inert.
- **New Item Studio: Import through the Builder now opens; the placement dialog's numbers now move the box.** With the catalogue backend the mesh patch flow needs the archive worker's prepared dependencies for the *selected* row, so the studio's hand-off found none and stopped with a status-bar line nobody read. `start_new_item_model_import` now selects the template's mesh in the Archive Browser (filtering the tree to it when it is off screen), waits for the worker to prepare its dependencies, then starts Import Mesh; when either does not come it says so in a message box that names the file to select by hand, and the flow's own dependency refusal is a message box while the studio is waiting. Separately, `DotNetPreviewHostFrame.set_alignment_preview_transform` sent only the `placement` numbers, which the helper ignores once a scene has an authoritative frame (it draws `roles.editable.model_matrix`), so a scale or offset typed into the placement dialog moved nothing; the host now composes that matrix (S·R·T, row-vector) and the role's world bounds from the placement, so the box and the particles follow the numbers as they follow the gizmo. Also: before the effects are indexed the dialog's box was a metre cube; it is now the effect's own bounding box read from its binary; the dialog says what the wire, the origin (the hand; blade toward -z, pommel +z) and the box are; the stats page names its columns in words (**Attack (DDD)**, Defence (DPV), Evasion (DDV), Guard rate...) and says what this template's row carries and that attack speed and criticals are perks and character, not the item; the Model step says how hand placement works (align to the template's wire in the Builder). Second pass: the button is **Import a model file...** and asks for the source first, a glTF, GLB, OBJ or DAE file or a zip holding one, from anywhere on disk (the folder is remembered in `ui/new_item_import_dir`), then selects the template's mesh in the Archive Browser, waits for its dependencies and opens the Builder over it with that file as the preset setup, so the reader never has to find the template's mesh or the file dialog inside the browser. And the orange box is gone from the placement dialog by default: the box mesh's faces are a cutout at opacity 0 (every fragment clipped), so the viewport shows the item's wire, the gizmo and the particles; **Show the effect's box** switches the viewport to its wire display mode to draw the bounding box's edges when the reader wants the reach.

### Changed
- **New Item Studio: the stat range warning names the crash.** A stat value far outside what shipped equipment carries has now crashed the game on purchase (an item with AttackSpeedRate at 10,000 against the game's own 30 to 90 million, seen in game 2026-08-19), so the Stats step and the plan say that rather than calling it unproven.
- **New Item Studio reads at a glance: tones, one sentence per step, no page-high blanks.** `cdmw/ui/new_item/ui_kit.py` is the studio's shared vocabulary: four tones (green settled, amber wants a decision or an in-game check, red blocks the plan, blue differs from the template), a `NoteLabel` that carries one, a `DetailsToggle` that folds background prose away, `intro_label` for the one plain sentence under each step's bold title, and `compact_table_height` so a two-level ladder is two lines tall. The left rail is now the step list as tall as its seven lines plus **Your item so far**: one tinted line per step (template, name, model, stats, perks and effect, shop, plan) with a legend, so what still needs a decision is visible from any step; the footer says "Step n of 7". Every step page opens with what it decides; a step's frame ends where its content ends. Per step: Template gets "The chosen template"; Identity a "Checks" group with red Blocked / amber Note lines and a green "Nothing blocks the plan"; Model and icon splits into Model and Icon groups, the two long paragraphs of import background fold under **Import tips**, and the two checkboxes say what they do in one line ("Use the game's plain PBR shaders for the imported textures (recommended)", "When sheathed on the back, draw the imported model"); Stats and prices becomes "Stats and shop prices per level" (table sized to its rows, blue edited cells in the ladder and the base prices) with **Sell for one copper**, **Back to the template's values** and **More: scale all, set all, add a level**, and a "Base price and stack" group; Perks and effect names the rows ("Start from a preset", "Or any shipped effect", "Index effects (once, about a minute)", "Where it sits: scale, offset", "How it looks: colour, brightness x, particle size x, spawn rate x, lifetime x", "Drop the colour") and folds the how-it-works paragraph; Shop and item groups gets a Shop group whose note is amber while nothing sells the item and green when a line does, and a groups list that no longer swallows the page; Output is three numbered groups (Build the plan with a tinted state line: not built / ready with counts / blocked; What the plan will change; Write it: "Mod folder for", "Write mod folder", install "straight into the game (asks first, keeps a backup)") with the in-game checklist folded. The localisation extractor learned the kit's sinks (`note`, `set_note`, `tinted`, `intro_label`, `NoteLabel`, `DetailsToggle`), so every tinted line is in the catalogs; 91 strings, 13 catalogs synced. The **Shop** field is no longer free text: a fixed list of every shop in the tables (`store_choices`), the player's camp shops first and green (`Store_Camp_Equipment` is the camp's equipment merchant, the base's shop where every check so far was bought), each line naming its place, kind and how many lines it sells, `Store_Camp_Equipment` preselected; `PlacementPanel.choose_store(name)` selects by table name and refuses unknown ones. Second pass from the app's own dark theme: the muted intro and legend text is now computed from the palette (`step_style`, `muted_color`; a fixed `palette(dark)` was invisible on dark), the rail is 300 px with bold labels and long values shortened in the middle, the Checks on step 2 follow every change the rail follows (a chosen shop no longer leaves a stale "no shop placement" note) and list what blocks first, a template pick suggests a unique internal name (`<Template>_New`, `suggest_internal_name`) instead of leaving the reader on "already exists", the shop rows are a form and **Entry to replace** and the unlock checkbox only show when replacing, and an added line gets its own green note; the rail shows Perks and Effect on separate lines.

### Added
- **The resident viewport draws the effect: an approximate particle preview.** The .NET helper reads `effect_preview.json` beside a package's mesh (`tools/dotnet_mesh_editor_experiment/EffectParticlePreview.cs`) and simulates it on the CPU (`EffectEmitterSimulation`): bursts by rate while the emitter spawns (a looping one always, a one-shot for its `spawn_time` and then a pause, so it replays), particles pushed by their force over mass (a mass under a twentieth of a kilogram treated as that, so a 0.011 kg ember stays in frame), damped, speed-capped, aged; sprite quads facing the camera in the effect's frame, which is the placement of the package's editable submesh (the box the gizmo moves), sized by the scale curve, tinted by the colour curve normalised to its peak (a dim HDR fire still shows), faded by the alpha curve, the flipbook frame (`_sequenceCountX/Y`) advanced over life, stretched along velocity when the emitter says so; lightning-style emitters as a jittered ribbon along the emitter box's long axis, re-rolled every 60 ms, with a soft halo. `D3D11MaterialViewport.EffectParticles.cs` uploads the quads to a dynamic buffer, draws them after the transparent pass with new `VSParticle`/`PSParticle` stages (sprite DDS through the material set's DDS reader on t11, luminance-or-alpha mask; a soft disc without a texture; additive or non-premultiplied blend by the emitter's `BLEND_MODE`; depth-tested, no depth write) and keeps a 30 Hz pump running while a preview is loaded; the `package_load_applied` ack carries `effect_preview` (emitters, textures, particles), the helper announces `effect_particle_preview_v1`, and `CDMW_EFFECT_PARTICLE_DEBUG=<file>` logs the drawn bounds every 30 frames. The placement dialog says which it is showing: the particles when the helper has the layer, the box until then. Proven against the archives: the fire attach loop with a blue look drew blue sprites in the box, the titan lightning drew 3.8 m green bolts (the emitter box's long axis, hair-thin at that effect's 22 m box, which is what it is).
- **New Item Studio: the effect's particle description, for a viewport that draws it.** `cdmw/services/effect_preview_model.py` reads a decoded effect into an approximate simulation the resident viewport can run on the CPU (`EffectPreview`, one `EmitterPreview` per emitter): burst size and bursts per second (`_spawnCountMax`, `_spawnTermMin/Max`), lifetime, loop, cap; the spawn shape (points sampled on the `_spawnMeshSurfaceFileName` mesh when it reads, else a spread from `_particleAverageDistance`); force, damping and speed limit; scale and z rotation; scale over life (curve id 5) and alpha over life (curve id 2), each read by shape and said so; colour over life from the colour curve (id 21) with the temperature ramp (`_temperatureColorSpline`, four splines of `(x, y)` points, x the temperature over 1000) folded in and `_temperatureBrightness` applied, so a fire that colours by temperature previews orange and a look-edited one previews in its colour; the sprite texture and blend mode; a beam kind for lightning-style emitters. Values are read in a fixed order: the effect's embedded override (positional entries through `EmitterLayout`), the emitter file, then the `_renderGroupPreset` it names (whose emissive colour wins unless `_overridePresetColor` is set); each curve's `_componentCount` says how many channels it has, and missing emitter files or spawn meshes are notes, not errors. `preview_effect_from_snapshot` reads everything out of the archives with the draft's `EffectLook` applied first (effect, emitters, render presets), `write_effect_preview` puts it beside the mesh in the placement package as `effect_preview.json` (schema 1) with the DDS sprites copied under `effect_textures/` and a `texture_files` map, and the placement dialog gets the description from the controller (`effect_preview_for_placement`), lists the emitters next to the viewport (kind, rate, life, loop, blend, texture, peak colour) and says the viewport draws them once its particle layer lands, the box until then. This is the honest ceiling the viewer's particle layer builds on: fire that looks like that fire, roughly where it will be, not the game's pixels.
- **Effect binaries decode to the byte.** `cdmw/core/effect_binary.py` reads the game's `.pae` (effects) and `.paem` (emitters): a 16-byte `PARC` header around the reflection container the prefab decoder already parses (type table, string pool, blob), walked here by a grammar that closes all 6,855 shipped effect and emitter files exactly (`tests/test_effect_binary.py`, corpus gate behind `CDMW_EFFECT_CORPUS=1`). What the walk established: an object is `u16 mask width, mask, u16 type index, u8 override flag`, then (below the root) `u64 owner, u32 self pointer` (container-relative, equal to its own offset + 4), a name record and a trailing `u32` length; container members (inline arrays, object lists, string lists) always write a null byte and the presence mask does not gate them; members with attribute bit 0x80 (`_segmentList`, `_isEditLock`...) are editor-only and never written; an effect embeds the emitters it uses as types named by their file path, with `override` objects as deltas over them (an override curve with an empty mask keeps the base curve); baked curves are half-float sample arrays. Every inline value comes back with its absolute offset (`ReflectValue`), so colours, intensities, sizes, counts and flags can be written back in place (`write_value`); `EffectDocument.emitter_names()`/`resources()` list what an effect instances and draws. This is the ground for placing and editing weapon effects from the studio: `EffectData._boundingBoxMin/Max`, `EmitterRenderData._emissiveColor`, `_emissiveIntensityExponent`, `EmitterSpawnData._spawnCountMin/Max`, `_lifeTimeMin/Max`, `_scaleMin/Max` are all named, typed and addressable now.
- **New Item Studio: the effect catalogue.** `cdmw/services/effect_catalogue.py` reads every shipped effect once through the new decoder (about a minute for the 6,109, cached as JSON under the archive cache root and keyed by the effect population) into `EffectFacts`: the authoring name, the emitters it instances, the textures and meshes those draw, its bounding box, whether it loops (the root's flags, or an emitter with loop count −1), lights. The studio's Perks and effect panel gets an **Index effects** button; after it the effect filter matches every word against stem, emitters, textures and meshes ("fire circle", "firefly", "vector_chaos"), and a facts line under the list says what the chosen effect draws and how big its box is at the chosen scale, so a 2.5 m burning loop is not put on a sword at 1.0 by guesswork.
- **New Item Studio: place the effect in the viewport.** `cdmw/services/effect_placement_preview.py` turns the chosen effect's bounding box into a translucent orange box mesh (24 vertices, its own material blended at 35%) and packages it for the resident .NET viewport with the item's mesh as the reference (`comparison_mode` overlay, `interaction_mode` placement), and `cdmw/ui/new_item/effect_placement_dialog.py` hosts it: **Place in viewport...** on the Perks and effect panel opens the dialog with the item's mesh (the imported model, else the template's own), the effect's box, and the current scale and offset; the placement gizmo's drags come back as deltas that the dialog adds to its numbers (Move: offset along the item's axes; Scale: a uniform scale, the mean of the three axes), the size line says how big the box is at that scale, and accepting writes scale and offset into the panel and the draft. `DotNetPreviewHostFrame.set_alignment_gizmo_tool` switches the gizmo between move, rotate and scale from Python (it was only ever set from the scene file). Without the viewport the dialog still edits the numbers.
- **New Item Studio: the effect's look, edited in place on a clone of the item's own.** `cdmw/core/effect_edit.py` applies an `EffectLook` (`cdmw/domain/new_item/spec.py`: a colour, and factors on brightness, particle size, spawn rate and lifetime) to an effect or emitter binary without moving a byte: `EmitterRenderData._emissiveColor`/`_color` take the chosen hue at the old colour's peak component, `_emissiveBrightness`/`_brightness`, `EmitterSimulationData._scaleMin/_scaleMax`, `_particleScaleMax` and the bounding boxes, `EmitterSpawnData._spawnCountMin/_spawnCountMax/_maxParticleCount` and `_lifeTimeMin/_lifeTimeMax` are multiplied. Renaming keeps every string the same length (`same_length_stem`: the shipped stem's tail becomes `_n<item key>`), so the type table, the string pool and every self pointer stay put; an effect names its emitters twice (as path-typed embedded objects and as `_emitterDataName` values) and both are replaced. The planner (`_clone_effect_for_look`) clones the effect and the emitters it instances under those stems, edits them, adds them, and grafts the clone; the manifest records the look and what it touched. The Perks and effect panel gets a **Look** row: a colour button (`QColorDialog`, "as shipped" to drop it) and Brightness/Particle size/Spawn rate/Lifetime factors; the rules bound the factors to 0.05..20 and the colour to 0..1, and warn that which of these the game honours is unproven until the first in-game look.
- **Effect look, second pass: presets and the override flag; `_sp` red channel.** The first in-game look at an edited fire (blue, twice as bright, half the size) drew the shipped fire: an emitter's colour and brightness are its render preset's (`_renderGroupPreset` -> `effect/binary__/renderpreset/<name>.parg`, the same reflection container, walked to the byte) unless `EmitterRenderData._overridePresetColor` says otherwise, and the fire ember ships that flag false. A colour edit now sets the flag wherever the file carries it, and the planner clones the render and simulation presets the effect and its emitters name (same-length stems, `rename_string_values` on the exact string values) and applies the look to them too. Whether the game then honours the clone is the next in-game look. String values now report the span of their characters, so a same-length write lands on them. Also: `encode_sp_from_png` writes 255 in R (the shipped `_sp` textures do; a glTF map's R is occlusion or nothing) after the Jade Sword drew grey.
- **New Item Studio, one step at a time.** The seven panels no longer stack in one long scroll: a step list on the left (Template, Identity, Model and icon, Stats and prices, Perks and effect, Shop and item groups, Output) shows one panel at a time filling the pane, with Back/Next and a one-line summary of the item so far along the bottom (`NewItemStudioTab.show_step` jumps to a step). Lists that were capped now grow with the page. **Stats and prices** say what they are in plain words (one row per enhancement level, stat columns are the item's numbers, price columns what the shop charges at that level), get a **Sell for one copper** button (every level price and base price to 1) and fold the scale/set/add-a-level/reset controls and the own-enhancement-rows switch behind an Advanced toggle. **The icon can be captured from the resident viewport** (`cdmw/ui/new_item/icon_capture_dialog.py`: the item's mesh, the imported model else the template's, in the .NET viewport; orbit and zoom, Capture takes the 512 x 512 frame with grid and gizmo hidden, the way the Model Library's icon capture does; accepting sets it as the generated icon's source). Also: the Verdict Axe, Epitaph of Calamity and Artifact Sword were installed straight into the archives from a three-item pack in one write (2026-08-19 01:09).
- **New Item Studio reads the archives itself when the shell's list is empty.** The standalone archive catalogue backend (the default since the load-time pass) shows the Archive Browser without ever filling the window's legacy `archive_entries` list, so the studio's "Read the archives" answered "the archive list is empty" on a freshly built app even after a scan (2026-08-19). With no entries and a game folder set, the studio's snapshot worker now lists the package tables under that folder itself (`list_archive_entries`, about ten seconds for the shipped game, progress in the status line) and reads the tables from them; only with no folder either does it ask for one.
- **New Item Studio: a shop line that never runs out.** A `StockData.count` is how many the shop has: 1 on most equipment lines (sold once, then "0 in stock", which is what the checks hit after one purchase), 10-60 on arrows, 0xFFFFFFFF on the bank's gold bars and the leather shops' Theona Checkerspot, which never run out. `Placement.stock_count` (`UNLIMITED_STOCK` for that value) is written by `swap_stock_item`/`insert_stock_entry` (`count=`), `set_stock_count` sets it on a line already in the shop, and the Shop panel gets **Unlimited stock**, on by default. Also: an inserted stock entry sold in game (the blue fire look 3 line, 2026-08-18), so the insert form drops its "unproven" wording and warning; and the blue fire drew blue, so the effect look's colour is proven (the colour curve and the temperature ramp are what the game reads).
- **Effect look, third pass: a fire's colour is a curve and a ramp.** The game's own blue fire (`cdem_standard_fire_large_fire_blue_004a`) differs from the orange one in two places besides its textures: the baked colour-over-life curve (`EmitterCurveData` with `_splineID` 21, the only four-component curve id shipped: 128 samples of R, G, B and temperature as half floats; orange (0.647, 0.079, 0.001, 1000) becomes (0.002, 0.048, 0.12, 0)) and the material's temperature ramp (`_temperatureColorSpline`: four splines R, G, B, intensity over normalised temperature). A colour edit now recolours both wherever the graph carries them: every curve sample and every ramp point keeps its brightness (the peak channel) and takes the chosen hue, the temperature channel and the intensity spline are left alone, tangents scale with their point, and `_precomputeEmissiveLightLinearColor` (the light an emitter casts) follows. An effect overrides an emitter's curves and material parameters by position (an override record names no `_splineID` and no `_name`), so `emitter_layout_of` reads an emitter file's positions (`EmitterLayout`) and the planner hands the clones' layouts to the effect edit, which recolours the positional overrides too (fire1 overrides its third emitter's ramp that way). A ramp point the file leaves at its default has no bytes to write and stays; a fire's temperature is constant over life, so the top of the ramp is what shows. An intensity edit multiplies the material's `_temperatureBrightness` alongside `_emissiveBrightness`, since a temperature-driven fire's brightness is the ramp's.
- **New Item Studio, phase 11: the baseline a helm inherits, and where a sword sits in the hand.** A helmet imported over the Unyielding Warrior's Plate Helm hid the head under it; the same model over the Northern Fighter's Plate Helm keeps the face drawn. The difference is the template's part prefabs, which the clone keeps: the Northern Fighter's occupies `CD_Helm_Small` and `CD_Item_Hair` and its prefab carries a second SkinnedMeshComponent with a helmet hair mesh (`cd_phm_00_hair_00_0505`), the Unyielding Warrior's and Canta helms occupy `CD_Helm_Small` alone (which field hides the head is not identified; the pappt record, the pac_xml and the row's word before the stat block were all ruled out). `cdmw/services/new_item_baseline.py` reads what a template's owned prefabs carry (slots, companion meshes, component count) and the studio's Template panel now lists it, so the baseline is chosen knowingly; the Model panel and About say which shipped helms do what. Placement, measured against the shipped meshes: the character faces −z (the face and eyebrow meshes sit at z −0.17..−0.10, the hair behind them), the weapon's origin is the hand, and on Reckleeman's and Ziane's swords the guard's handle-side edge sits 0.10 m in front of it with the handle behind; the Full Import preset's `grid_flat` alignment lays a model on the ground plane and aligns its longest axis with the template's, which for a horned helmet meant a rotated, shrunk helmet in the ground, so a helm goes through the Builder's manual placement (scale, no rotation, origin at the head). Live: the four imported swords rebuilt with their guard edge where the templates' are and the blade centred in thickness (offset z relative to the preset: wolf −0.08, Frostmourne −0.20, King Arthur +0.08, Buster −0.08), and the Nord helmet re-imported over the Northern Fighter's helm at scale 0.01 with its origin at (0, 1.745, −0.03), taking the old one's shop line. Also: the `Encoding a solid _sp` progress line from the last commit had missed the localisation manifest; it is in now.
- **The game samples a mesh's texture V from the top of the image.** Rasterising a shipped weapon (`cd_phm_01_axe_0001_03.pac` with its `_baseColorTexture`) with the UVs exactly as `parse_pac` returns them gives a coherent axe head only when V=0 is the top row of the DDS; the glTF convention, so a glTF's V belongs in the PAC as it is. The importer stores glTF/GLB/OBJ/DAE V as 1-v (`scene_gltf_geometry.py`, the "Flip V normalisation"), and the static replacement writes that into the mesh, so an imported model draws with its textures mirrored along itself unless the Builder's Flip V is ticked; two imported swords showed exactly that in game (2026-08-18), and their meshes were rebuilt with the flip and patched in place. The New Item Studio's Model panel says so; the root fix (which sources need the 1-v, the previews and tests that assume it) is a task of its own.
- **New Item Studio, phase 10: what the third in-game look said.** The two plain-PBR swords showed five empty sockets and no perk lines, and the fourth perk had been dropped on the earlier items: the byte after the stat block's `0x11` marker is the **socket item count the game reads**, equal to `_socketItemList`'s length on all 5,767 shipped rows with a stat block, and the byte after it is 1 on every row with a socket slot; the encoder kept the template's stale values (0 on Reckleeman's greatsword, 3 on Wolf's Fang) and now writes both from the lists it serialises (the corpus gate holds the rule). The lightning-loop and fire checks reached metres past the blade: the grafted `EffectComponent` took an identity `_offsetTransform`, and the studio now has an **effect scale and offset** (`NewItemSpec.effect_scale`, `effect_offset`; the presets carry a starting scale, 0.2 for the titan's lightning, 0.6 for the fire sweep, the shipped spear's own is 0.7). The shipped scabbard drew beside an imported greatsword: a weapon's sheathed look is a part of its own (the `_IN` stems), usually **borrowed** from another item (Reckleeman's greatsword borrows `cd_phm_02_sword_0001_in`, the sword-in-scabbard model; Hwando's own is `cd_phm_02_sword_0036_in`; eight shipped two-hand swords have none), and the studio kept borrowing it. `SheathedModel.OWN_MODEL` (the default for an imported model, a checkbox on the Model panel) clones the borrowed `_IN` records under the item's stem (`<stem>_in`, `_r_in`/`_l_in` by hand), copies their prefabs re-pathed to the imported mesh, adds the strings and swaps the row's hashes, so the sheathed sword is the imported one. All three fixes were applied to the live items in place (perk counts on every row of ours, J at 0.2, H at 0.6, own `_in` parts on the plain-PBR pair). Also learnt: the `Item_Skill_AbyssGear_*` gems are Abyss skills (Volcanic Eruption, Frost Hail, Orbs of Lightning, Storm Fang, Groundsurge, Tempest of Destruction, Wind Slash...) and shipped items embed them by default, so an elemental ability is a perk; the plain-PBR pair carries five each.
- **New Item Studio, phase 9: the imported textures as the source painted them, and the element presets.** The user's imported swords drew with the wrong textures in game. Root cause: the Builder's Full Import keeps the template's layered `SkinnedMeshStandard_Ver2` material and fits the imported PBR textures into it (the albedo through an overlay slot, the roughness clamped towards matte, the game's own detail layers drawn over the lot). The game's shader for texture-driven surfaces is plain `SkinnedMeshStandard`, which 274 shipped one-hand and 116 two-hand weapon materials use: `_baseColorTexture` (DXT1, DXT5 with alpha), `_normalTexture` (BC5U) and `_materialTexture`, the `_sp` map whose G is roughness and B metalness (the shipped DXIL, as the renderer already reads it); `SkinnedMeshEmissive` is the same plus `_emissiveIntensityTexture`, `_emissiveColor` and `_emissiveIntensity`. The `.pac` binary names no shader and no texture, so the material a submesh draws with is the sidecar's alone. `cdmw/core/pac_xml_standard_material.py` finds every material wrapper of a `.pac_xml` and rewrites the named ones to that shape (the well-known parameters take the countdown `ItemID`s of the most common shipped pattern, which shipped files disagree on and the game therefore cannot depend on; the wrapper's attributes, the file's newline convention and its indentation survive; the rest of the file is byte for byte). `cdmw/services/new_item_materials.py` is the studio's route over it: the wrappers the import owns (any texture among the Builder's side files) get the Builder's base colour and normal, an `_sp` encoded from the source's own metallic/roughness map when the source is known (glTF packs G roughness, B metalness, the `_sp` layout, so the pixels go through unchanged) and the Builder's mask otherwise (with a warning that its roughness was clamped), and an emissive encoded from the source as a BC4 intensity map with the colour the lit pixels average to (a blue rune stays blue) when the source glows; wrappers sharing a base texture share a source (a cloned section), and Builder textures no wrapper names any more are dropped. `NewItemSpec.material_route` (`PLAIN_PBR` by default for an imported model, `BUILDER` keeps the sidecar as it came), a checkbox on the Model panel, and the Builder hand-off now carries the scene import the build ran from so the studio can find the source textures (`start_new_item_model_import` -> `_new_item_model_scene`). The texture registry learnt the tags: shipped base colours are tagged 12 in `dwReserved2` (13 with alpha), `_sp` 12, `_n`/`_disp`/`_ma`/`_mg`/`_emi` 4, `_m` 5, and `register_dds` reuses a shipped header only when its tag matches too, else writes a header row with the right tag, so a base colour never lands on a mask's row. **Effects**: `cdmw/domain/new_item/effects.py` curates the shipped effects named for weapons and elements (fire, frost, lightning loops and attach loops, glows, auras, trails, the Antumbra weapon set) as **element presets** on the Perks and effect panel, the fire one marked proven (it drew flames on the sword in the shop); on-hit effects (an explosion when the blade lands) are combat data, not a prefab, and stay outside the studio. Both swords were installed again with the plain-PBR route beside the Builder-route ones, so the two can be compared in the shop.
- **New Item Studio, phase 8: what the second in-game look said, and the four things it took.** The user's swords showed the placeholder icon, three of four perks, a "Knowledge" lock in the shop, and one-handed grips; each is a finding. **Icons** need a line in the UI's icon registry, `ui/xml/texture/cd_item_icon.xml` (a `<Texture Name=... Filename=... />` per icon, 8,368 shipped, LZ4 + ChaCha20 in 0012): a file in the archive and registered in `meta/0.pathc` but absent there still draws as the bag. `cdmw/core/item_icon_registry.py` appends the line shaped like the template's, and the planner patches the registry with every generated icon. **Perks** are capped by the row's socket *slots* (`_addSocketMaterialList`, one entry per slot with its opening cost: 500, 1000, 2000 copper on Wolf's Fang; five on the boss sword): a fourth gem on a three-slot row is dropped, so `encode_stat_block(add_socket_materials=...)` and `socket_slots_for` grow the slots to fit the gems, priced like the shipped progression. **Shop lines** may carry an unlock requirement, the optional 13-byte block being `u32 item, u8 1, u64`: the collection prop (bottles, trophies, ceramics) whose knowledge the buyer must hold, 1,856 of the 6,379 shipped stock entries; the shop shows "Knowledge" until then. `StockEntry.requirement_item_key`, `swap_stock_item(keep_requirement=...)`, `insert_stock_entry(keep_requirement=...)`; `Placement.keep_requirement` (default false: the new item sells freely, and the summary says which knowledge was dropped), the placement panel names each line's requirement and offers to keep it, and the golden replay states the spike kept the requirement. **Textures** of a shape the registry has never seen get a header row of their own in `meta/0.pathc` (the file's DDS header with `dwReserved1` zeroed and `dwReserved2` the usage tag every shipped registry header carries, 4 on most character textures, plus the DX10 extension when present) with block infos computed as mip sizes, so full-size imported textures register instead of being refused. Two-handedness comes from the template (a two-hand sword template gives a two-hand item), and the shop price from the level-0 buy prices. Also learnt: the fire check's grafted `EffectComponent` draws (flames on the sword in the shop), so grafted effects work; the lightning one was a one-shot action effect and flashed once, and a `_loop` effect is the persistent form.
- **New Item Studio, phase 7: perks and a weapon effect, from the screen.** The studio gains a **Perks and effect** panel. Perks are the Abyss Gear socket items an item row embeds by default (the tooltip's "Insight I", "Malicebane I" lines): keep the template's, or pick up to eight from the whole gem catalogue by English name (every item of the gems' item type, 190 in the shipped table), with the shipped maximum of four named. A weapon effect is one of the shipped effect binaries (`effect/binary__/releasebin/<stem>.pae`, filterable), grafted into the item's own prefabs as an `EffectComponent`, the way the shipped thrown lightning spear carries its aura; choosing one gives the item a model family of its own even on the template's mesh. Underneath: `cdmw/core/prefab_component_graft.py` copies a component element from a donor prefab into a target that never declared its type. The donor's declarations of the component and of every type the element's pointer headers name are appended to the target's type table (so the string pool, data header and blob move), the element's own type indices are remapped (every pointer record sits behind a `u16 width, mask (width bytes), u8 typeIndex, u16 0` header, the element's name record included), the element's true bytes run from that header to the end of its name-length field, pointers are recomputed from their new positions, the effect path is replaced at any length with the spanning length fields adjusted, and the result is read back and refused unless the walk completes and the target's strings survive in order. Getting there fixed the decoder: the element header's leading u16 is the mask's byte width, not a marker with a fixed u16 mask, so a three-byte mask carries member bits 16..23; the spear's `EffectComponent` selects member 18 (`_effectTarget`) and walked 33 bytes short, and 14 more of the 15,557 shipped character prefabs now complete, none lost. `NewItemSpec.effect` (`<stem>.level.effect`), rules that refuse an unknown stem and warn that a grafted effect is unproven, the snapshot's effect stems, the planner's `plan_texture_registry`-style step for prefabs, and gates: synthetic prefabs shaped like the shipped ones (name-length fields, pointee headers, the trailing byte after an object member) and a real_game graft of the spear's element onto forty sword prefabs. Two check items, `Wolf's Fang (fire check)` and `(lightning check)`, were installed into the live game with grafted effects.
- **New Item Studio, phase 6b: what the game said, and what it took to answer.** The first in-game look at the four checks (Tranan's shop, the Base Camp quartermaster) showed nameless items and a bag for an icon, and each of those was a finding. **Names:** the game derives an item's localisation keys from its id, `(id << 32) | 0x70` for the name and `| 0x71` for the description (every one of the 6,573 shipped rows), and the fourteen `.paloc` tables keep their numeric keys in that ascending order; the allocator's invented `43005292<id>1/2` keys were carried in the row and never looked up, so the 2026-08-17 spike had blank names too. `localization_keys()` now derives the shipped form, `add_localization_entries` slots new numeric keys into the table's order (an earlier mod's out-of-order tail is left alone), `NewItemSpec` validation warns on any other key, and a real_game gate holds every shipped row to the rule. **Icons:** a `.dds` in the archive but not in `meta/0.pathc` draws as the placeholder bag: the game consults that texture registry first. `cdmw/core/pathc_format.py` owns it now (head, 648 texture headers, 285,224 ascending path checksums with parallel entry rows, 12 collision rows, the name blob; byte-exact on the shipped file), `register_texture` adds a row like a reference texture's and `register_dds` under whichever shipped header the file's own DDS header equals (reserved header words the engine tags are left out of the comparison), the planner registers every new `.dds` in `plan_texture_registry`, the plan carries the rewritten registry as a `MetaFileWrite`, and `ArchiveMutationService` writes such loose index files after the archive chain verifies, inside the same backup, so a restore brings the registry back with the tables. **Perks:** the tooltip's perk lines are the row's default Abyss Gear socket items (190 gems in the shipped table: Destruction, Insight, Swift, the `-bane` damage gems, resistances, Fortune...; shipped rows carry up to four); `encode_stat_block(socket_items=...)` writes them, `NewItemSpec.socket_items` chooses them, and the rules check they are item keys and warn when nothing shipped embeds them or there are more than four. **Stems:** an item that only generated an icon under a stem now owns that stem for allocation. The four checks were restored and reinstalled with the fixes, plus a fifth, `Wolf's Fang (perks check)` with four level-III gems, and the user's two glTF swords were imported headless through the Full Import Model Replacement preset and installed as new items with perks and registered icons. `MetaFileWrite` joins the mutation domain; `ArchivePatchResult.meta_paths` reports what was written. A persistent fire or lightning visual on a weapon is an `EffectComponent` in the prefab (the shipped thrown lightning spear carries one); the prefab codec has no component writer, so that is a later phase.
- **Placement Studio: a move is one operation, and only the operations you select are packaged.** The package path serialized the whole edit session, so an earlier shield move and an earlier one-handed animation swap shipped inside the two-handed sword mod the user had just finished. Every edit now carries the operation that recorded it (`operation_id`, `equipment_unit_id`, `scope_kind`, `created_order` on `Command`), one accepted dialog opens one `OperationHandle` and commits or rolls it back whole, and `Recent actions` shows one row per operation with undo, redo and discard acting on all of it. Packaging asks what to include, defaults to the latest operation alone, and builds from `EditSession.isolated_session`: a fresh session over the pinned vanilla baseline with only the chosen operations replayed, so an unselected experiment is structurally unable to reach the output rather than filtered out of it. `tools/placement_studio/preflight.py` then proves it, blocking on a descriptor row, socket file or animation path outside what the selected operations declared, a target family that is not the equipment unit's own, a required sheath row left unhandled without a confirmed exception, a shared child socket changed in place, a route to an undefined socket, a body socket and child socket in different destination zones, an unreviewed borrowed orientation, a placement operation with no route command, and two selected operations that settle the same field differently. Every package writes `operation_manifest.json` beside its metadata, and `placement_studio inspect-package <dir>` reads a built package back, parses the descriptor and socket diffs against vanilla, counts the animation family of every clip path, and exits non-zero when the files and the manifest disagree in either direction.
- **Placement Studio resolves the whole equipment unit, so a sword's sheath moves with it.** The item dropdown could select one descriptor row while the animation pairing and the child-socket work still used the previously selected weapon asset, which permitted a mixed operation: one row moved while another weapon's animations were rewritten. `PlacementSession.resolve_equipment_unit` now returns one validated `EquipmentUnit` (primary row, linked rows, handedness, exact target and donor animation families, the descriptor and socket files it may write) and raises rather than falling back when the row and the asset disagree on model or handedness, or when the row's child sockets are none the asset defines. Linked rows are found three ways, none of them a hardcoded `Sword_IN`: the row's own `WeaponCasePart` link, the reverse of that link, and a companion row sharing the stem under a known suffix, classified by `link_role` into sheath, scabbard, quiver, holster or auxiliary. `move_operation.plan_move` resolves the whole move before any of it is applied and `apply_move` records it through one handle, so B4's all-or-none linked routing is a property of the transaction: a case row that cannot be safely oriented blocks the commit and leaves no partial weapon route behind. The weapon and its sheath get separate child-socket decisions in separate asset files, because their geometry and local axes differ.
- **New Item Studio, phase 6: enhancement rows of the item's own, and the four in-game checks installed.** `cdmw/core/multichangeinfo_table.py` reads `multichangeinfo.pabgb`, the table an item's `_multiChangeInfoList` points at: a row is `u32 key, u32 len, name, NUL, ...`, the enhancement rows are named `<item internal name>_<level>` and carry the item's own key 24 bytes after the name (17,032 of the 17,831 rows named that way; Ziane's sword lists fourteen, `Ziane_OneHandSword_0..13`, shared with no other sword). `find_multichange_keys` finds an item's list by its own shape (a u32 count and that many known keys before the stat block), `transition_rows_for` keeps the rows that name the item, and `clone_transition_rows` appends copies under new keys and names with the item key repointed and every other byte kept. `NewItemSpec.enhancement` chooses between sharing the template's rows (`TEMPLATE`, the default and the in-game-verified form; the rules say the item enhances through rows that name the template) and rows of its own (`OWN`, warned as unproven); the planner clones them before the ItemInfo row so the row's list is swapped to the new keys, and the studio's Stats panel offers the choice. The insert-into-shop rule that demanded a price is gone: a StoreInfo entry carries none, so the studio's insert path works. Four check items were installed into the live archives through the studio's own service, each with a backup, for the checks only the game can answer: `Wolf's Fang (icon check)` (a generated icon at a new path, no PATHC entry), `(ladder check)` (level 0 damage doubled and a twelfth level added), `(insert check)` (a whole new stock entry) and `(own rows check)` (fourteen cloned enhancement rows), all in `Store_Camp_Equipment`.
- **New Item Studio, phase 5: the plan is pinned to what the game accepted.** `tests/fixtures/new_item_golden/` (348 KB) holds the 2026-08-17 spike's pre-install source trimmed to what its two clones touch (the template's and swap victims' ItemInfo rows, the part stems' StringInfo rows, four part-prefab records, two store rows, eleven group rows, StatusInfo and EquipTypeInfo whole, each of the fourteen language tables cut to the template's entries, and the template's model family files) and the spike's own output (the two ItemInfo rows, both store rows, the two re-pathed prefabs, and the StringInfo rows, part-prefab records, group memberships, localisation records and new-file hashes as JSON). `tests/test_new_item_golden.py` lays the source out as a synthetic package, plans Clone A (template model, swapped into two shops) and Clone B (cloned model family) through `NewItemService.plan()` with the spike's keys and names, and asserts byte-identical ItemInfo rows, StringInfo rows, store rows and prefabs, identical part-prefab records beside the template's, identical group memberships and localisation records in all fourteen languages, and identical hashes for the copied mesh, sidecar and physics. It sits in the archive gate beside the corpus gates, so a game update that moves a field is caught by the corpus gates and a change to the pipeline that drifts from the accepted bytes is caught here.
- **New Item Studio, phase 4: the screen. A brand-new item can now be made end to end from the app.** `Assets > New Item Studio` (`cdmw/ui/new_item/`, lazy tool tab `new_item_studio`) reads the item, string, part-prefab, store, group and language tables once into the service's snapshot, then walks six panels: **Template** (a search over equipment items, or `Clone as new item...` from the Item Finder's detail panel), **Identity** (internal name, item key and model stem allocated unless set, names and descriptions per shipped language with English required), **Model and icon** (keep the template's model, or `Import through the Builder...`, which runs Import Mesh over the template's mesh and hands the Builder's result to the studio instead of writing it over the template; keep the template's icon or generate one from an image or a folder), **Stats and prices** (a grid over the template's enchant ladder with scale, flat, add-a-level and reset presets, base prices and stack count; only cells that differ from the template become edits), **Shop and item groups** (replace a shop entry, the in-game-proven form, or add one; the template's groups or an explicit list), and **Output** (`Build plan` shows the summary, warnings and every touched path without writing; `Write loose mod` lays it out for CDUMM, DMM or JMM; `Install into the game archives...` confirms, backs up and goes through `ArchiveMutationService`, and is refused while the game runs; then an in-game checklist). The panels edit a pure draft; a controller owns the service, the snapshot, the plan and one worker thread at a time and reports through signals, so the tab never touches an archive. The tab joins the shell's close sweep, the detachable-tool registry and the About page (English, Spanish, German), `ServiceContainer` gains `new_items`, and every string is in the localisation manifest and the fourteen catalogs. Headless gates drive a template-model plan, an imported-model plan and a real install through the panels against the synthetic package.
- **New Item Studio, phase 3: the service that turns a specification into archive changes, exports them as a loose mod, or installs them.** `cdmw/services/new_item_snapshot.py` reads every table a new item touches once (ItemInfo, StringInfo, the part-prefab table, StoreInfo, ItemGroupInfo, StatusInfo, EquipTypeInfo, the English localisation table, the model stems under `character/model`) into a `NewItemSnapshot`, and `build_context` projects it onto the domain's `NewItemContext` for one template, so validation is lookups. `cdmw/services/new_item_planning.py` composes the phase-2 owners into a `NewItemPlan`: StringInfo strings first, part-prefab records, the cloned ItemInfo row (with hash swaps for a new model family or a generated icon, the stat, buy-price and price edits through the rebuild, and the stack count), item groups (the template's, or an explicit list, the clone placed beside its template as the in-game-verified spike did), the store swap or insert, names and descriptions in every shipped language with an English fallback and the template's own text when none is given, and, for an imported model, the family files under the new stem: the Builder's mesh, its sidecar with the produced textures renamed after the new stem, the template's physics, both prefabs re-pathed to the new mesh (a second owned mesh such as a sheath is copied), and the generated icon. The plan is patches (whole table payloads), additions (new files), the same bytes as game-relative loose files with `new_paths`, summary lines, warnings that say what is unproven in game, and a manifest. `NewItemService` in `cdmw/services/new_item_service.py` validates, allocates (item key from 1990000, a stem when a new model or icon needs one, localisation keys from the item key), plans, builds a generated icon from an image through the icon generator, `export_loose` writes the plan through `finalize_mod_package_export` in the CDUMM, DMM or JMM layout with the new paths declared, and `install` goes through `ArchiveMutationService` (backup, preflight, apply, restore on failure), refusing without confirmation and while `CrimsonDesert.exe` is running. `cdmw/workers/new_item_workers.py` shapes the snapshot, plan, export and install as cancellable utility tasks. `cdmw/core/itemgroupinfo_table.py` is the last table owner: every one of the 1,597 shipped item-group rows decodes (sub-group keys, member item keys, a tail whose length its own count predicts) and round-trips byte for byte, and `add_group_members` grows a group. A synthetic package carrying the whole template family proves the plan end to end: applied through the real mutation path, the archive reads back as a valid snapshot in which the new item resolves its own model family; a real_game gate plans against the shipped tables. No screen yet: that is phase 4.
- **New Item Studio, phase 2: the format owners a brand-new item writes through, each proven on the shipped tables.** The ItemInfo stat block can now be rebuilt from its parse rather than only edited in place: `encode_stat_block` reproduces all 6,241 decoded shipped stat blocks byte for byte, and `rebuild_stat_block` with `level_with_stat`, `level_without_stat`, `level_with_buy_price`, `next_level_like`, `price_list_with` and their inverses adds and removes enchant levels, stats and prices while the row's prefix and tail stay where they were. `storeinfo.pabgb` has a row model in `cdmw/core/storeinfo_table.py`: the stock list decodes to `StockData` entries (item, stock index, order index, min/max price percent, buyable/sellable flags, the optional restore-item block and order-count records) with the 37/85-byte prefix and the 15-17-byte tail carried opaque; all 437 rows and 6,378 entries round-trip byte for byte, and `swap_stock_item` (in place, the form the game accepted on 2026-08-17), `insert_stock_entry` (renumbers indices, recounts the head, grows the row through `replace_table_row`) and `remove_stock_entry` edit them. `cdmw/core/item_model_family.py` resolves an item's model family from its row: the part-prefab stems the row hashes, the prefab and `.pac` each resolves to, which parts are the item's own and which are borrowed (Wolf's Fang draws `cd_phm_01_sword_0109_r/_l` and borrows `cd_phm_01_sword_0168_*_in_index01` for its sheath), the `.pac`/`.pac_xml`/`.hkx`/prefab/icon files by convention with an exists flag each, and the renamed paths for a new stem; every one of the 5,679 shipped rows that references a part resolves, and 151 of 159 one-hand weapons own both hands with every file present. `rewrite_prefab_paths_any_length` in `cdmw/core/prefab_binary_edit.py` retargets a prefab's mesh path with the full relocating rewriter when the walk is determined and falls back to the same-length path otherwise, refusing a length change it cannot relocate; the two Wolf's Fang prefabs take a longer stem. `add_localization_entries` and `entries_like` in `cdmw/core/paloc_format.py` append a new item's names and descriptions to a language table shaped like the template's own records, refusing duplicate keys, with `language_of_paloc_path` and `text_for_language` (English fallback) for the fourteen tables. `cdmw/core/item_icon_addition.py` builds a new item's icon at a path the archive does not ship, shaped like a reference icon (`ItemIcon_Prefab_<Stem>` string, its hash, `ui/texture/icon/<lower-case>.dds`, and the `ArchiveAddRequest`). None of the new writers is UI-facing yet; the capability manifest's `.pabgb` row records what is proven in game and what is not.
- **New Item Studio, phase 1: the specification of a new item, its validation, and how its identity is allocated.** `cdmw/domain/new_item/` is pure rules with no archive access: `NewItemSpec` (template, internal name, per-language names and descriptions, model source, icon source, stat, buy-price and price edits, stack count, shop placement, item groups; identity fields may be left to allocation), `validate_spec` for shapes and ranges, and `validate_against_context` for collisions and template fit against a read-only snapshot the service hands in (`NewItemContext`: item keys, names, StringInfo texts, part-prefab stems, model stems, stores and their stock, localisation keys, StatusInfo keys, item groups, plus capability flags so the rules refuse what the writers cannot do yet: adding stats a template lacks, inserting into a store). Every refusal is a stable code with a field and a message. `allocation.py` allocates item keys from `1990000..1999999` (the range the in-game-verified spike used), suggests a stem of the template's length (`cd_phm_01_sword_0109` -> `cd_phm_01_sword_9109`, counting up while taken), maps a template's owned prefab stems onto the new family, and derives localisation keys from the item key. `replace_table_row` in `cdmw/core/structured_binary_editor.py` splices one row of any `.pabgb`/`.pabgh` pair, shifting later directory offsets, so a rebuilt stat block or a grown store row can be written back; replacing a shipped row with itself is a no-op on both files (real_game gate). Plan: `docs/plans/active/new-item-studio.md`.
- **The mesh right-click menu offers one import again, and the Builder decides what kind it is.** `Replace Materials and Textures Only` was added as a third context-menu entry beside `Import Mesh` and `Full Import Model Replacement`, which asked the reader to choose an operation before they had seen the model. The context menu now carries `Import Mesh...` alone; Full Import keeps its Import panel button. The materials-and-textures-only operation itself is unchanged and still writes no mesh entry at all, which is why the target's geometry hash cannot move.
- **ItemInfo rows have a stat model: the enchant ladder, the price list and the row identity decode by field, with byte offsets, and can be edited in place or cloned.** `cdmw/core/iteminfo_row.py` reads the prefix every one of the 6,573 shipped rows shares (`_key`, `_stringKey`, `_isBlocked`, `_maxStackCount`, the `_itemName` LocalStringInfo sub-record, `_equipTypeInfo` as `hashlittle(lowercase equip type name, 0xC5EDE)`, `_occupiedEquipSlotDataList`), anchors `_itemDesc` on its unique `07 71` sub-record and reads `_itemType` (u16; 103 on every one-hand sword, 202 on every two-hand sword, one code per weapon class) and the developer memo after it, then locates the stat block by a composite signature: `DropDefaultData`'s socket item and socket-material lists, a 0x11 marker, `_enchantDataList` (per level: `_statList_DataDefinedStatic` as {StatusInfo key, i32 value}, the static-level list, `_buyPriceList`, `_equipBuffs`) and `_priceList`. The layout was recovered from the 115 `ItemInfo` member names CrimsonDesert.exe carries in its read-failure messages plus corpus measurement: 6,241 rows resolve a stat block, all 292 weapon ladders carry DDD (1000002) at level 0 and 2,170 of 2,173 armour ladders carry DPV (1000003), and the 332 rows without one have an empty ladder, an empty price list and no sockets. `set_stat_value`, `set_buy_price`, `set_price`, `set_max_stack_count` and `scale_stats` rewrite fixed-size values in place; `clone_iteminfo_row` re-keys a template (three places), renames it and swaps model hashes, and reproduces the 2026-08-17 in-game-verified Clone B row byte for byte. `_itemTier`, `_equipableLevel` and `_maxEndurance` are deliberately not claimed: their bytes are in the opaque spans until their positions are proven. `parse_status_names` reads `statusinfo.pabgb` so stat keys show as names.
- **The archive writer can add brand-new entries, not only replace shipped ones.** `apply_archive_mutations` and `add_archive_entries` in the new `cdmw/core/archive_entry_addition.py` take an `ArchiveAddRequest` (group index, virtual path, payload, storage flags copied from a sibling) and rebuild that group's PAMT through a full structural model: a flat name-block record, a file record inserted in byte order inside its folder's range, later folders shifted by one, the payload appended to the group's smallest `.paz`, the PAZ record, PAMT crc and PAPGT slot rewritten, and the rebuilt index re-read and diffed against the shipped one before it is written. Replacements keep the in-place record rewrite in `archive_patching.py`, whose backup, PAPGT rechain, verify and restore steps now sit in one shared runner both paths use; a group that receives additions carries its replacements in the rebuild so one backup covers both. New folders, encryption other than ChaCha20 and compression other than raw/LZ4 are refused before backup. `ArchiveMutationService` plans carry additions beside patches. In-game proof: on 2026-08-17 a `.pac`, `.pac_xml`, `.hkx` and two `.prefab` files added this way under existing folders loaded and rendered as a new one-hand sword.
- **`character/bin__/partprefabtable.pappt`, the table that turns a part-prefab stem into a file, is fully decoded and appendable.** `cdmw/core/pappt_format.py` reads the 15,524 part records (stem, prefab folder, sockets descriptor, tag, flag, part slots) and 2,618 head records, rebuilds the shipped file byte for byte, and inserts cloned records beside their template. Item rows never carry prefab paths, only `hashlittle(stem, 0xC5EDE)`; StringInfo turns the hash back into the stem and this table turns the stem into `character/bin__/prefab/<folder>/<stem>.prefab`, so a new weapon needs one record here per hand. The Format Explorer and capability manifest now list `.pappt` as read `full`, write `constrained`.
- **Whole rows can be appended to any `.pabgb`/`.pabgh` table pair, and StringInfo has a structural owner.** `append_table_rows` in `cdmw/core/structured_binary_editor.py` appends rows that start with their own key and grows the directory in the header's own count and key widths; `cdmw/core/stringinfo_table.py` parses `stringinfo.pabgb` (31,438 rows, each keyed by `hashlittle(text, 0xC5EDE)`) and `append_stringinfo_strings` makes a list of texts resolvable, adding only the missing ones. `.pabgb` moves to write `constrained` in the capability manifest.
- **Archive Browser has `Full Import Model Replacement...` again, in the Import menu and the mesh context menu.** The backend preset that forces the complete source-owned route (sidecar rebuild, source-owned bindings, unused-DDS pruning, missing base-colour slot, layer neutralization, inherited-response reset, source-size textures) has been in the repository the whole time, but no button launched it, so the only way to a real swap was the Builder's complete-swap checkbox. The action takes an OBJ, DAE, glTF, GLB, or model ZIP, opens the same setup preflight Import Mesh uses, and hands the Builder a selection that applies the preset at build time; the Builder freezes the routing checkboxes it forces and keeps Material Authority tuning and Parts & Routing editable, as before.
- **Edit Mesh now keeps exact original lineage for Face Delete, Loop Cut, and midpoint Subdivide, and can serialize a topology-changed submesh back into the original PAC's LOD0 without approximating anything.** A topology edit renumbers vertices and triangles, so the same-count source maps that made a rebuild safe stop describing it. Every editable submesh now opens as its own provenance, one original parent per vertex and one original face per triangle, and each admitted edit folds onto that anchor, so a chain of subdivisions stays original-relative rather than relative to the previous edit. The resident session composes that lineage before it mutates anything, carries it through the existing undo/redo snapshot, and counts it toward the unchanged 64-operation and 256 MiB history limits. A separate PAC writer then rebuilds only the LOD0 geometry section: a direct vertex keeps its original 40-byte record byte for byte, a derived vertex starts from a parent record and only after every parent agrees on every protected byte, and derived skin rows keep the complete bone set with no influence dropped to fit six slots. Descriptor bounds, every lower LOD, and every unknown section are copied unchanged and re-checked by hash after the finished file is reparsed. Anything the writer cannot do exactly is refused with a stable reason instead of approximated. Measured on twelve body and clothing PACs from the installed game covering 151,927 unique LOD0 edges, 89.4% of edges merge into six or fewer palette slots, but only 0.0072% have parent records that agree on every protected byte, so Loop Cut and midpoint Subdivide are exact where they apply and blocked on most stock geometry today; Face Delete derives no new vertices and is unaffected. The Checks panel gains one `Exact topology rebuild` row and names the stable blocker whenever a contract stops describing its own geometry, so an unavailable rebuild says why instead of simply staying unavailable.

### Docs
- **The two red drag gates in the real-game Mesh Editor proof were a harness misread, not a product defect, and `-Area mesh` is now 65 of 65.** `selected_geometry_only` compared 39 selected vertices against 203 changed ones, and `selected_projection_tracks_cursor` measured a zero screen delta for a 40 px drag. Neither number was wrong. The .NET helper answers `tool_state` from the selection push it has already applied, and that trails the gesture by one, so asking once samples the selection before last: the answer following the push for request 8 still reported request 4, which was the projection probe's 39 vertices, while the session already held the gesture's 203. The harness recorded the probe's selection as the one under test while the stroke deformed the gesture's, so both gates were comparing two different gestures. It now re-asks until the applied push has caught up with the newest `select_request`, which takes two asks, and records the expected and applied push ids, the attempt count, and whether they converged, so a run that does not converge says so instead of quietly sampling a stale selection. Selected and changed are then the same `{submesh 1: 141, submesh 2: 62}` and the projected delta is 40.0 px against a 40 px drag. This confirms rather than changes the existing claim that Move, Grab, Smooth, Inflate, and Pinch preserve the committed selection: the behaviour was already correct and the gate proving it was misreading. `docs/release-confidence-plan.md` records the measurement, the earlier root cause it supersedes, and the geometric theories that were built, gated, and reverted, so none of them is re-derived.

### Changed
- **The upside-down check reports instead of rotating, and a shared child socket is cloned rather than edited.** `_ensure_blade_hangs_down` measured the placed geometry and, when the far end of the mesh sat above the attachment point, added a half turn about Y on its own. The turn is usually the right one, since it is exactly what separates the hip child socket (identity) from the back one (0, 1, 0, 0) on every weapon in the corpus, but the check deciding *whether* to apply it is a heuristic: mesh origins, geometry distribution, attachment transforms and child-socket composition all differ by asset, so it could write an inverted orientation while the preview and the naming convention both said otherwise. `orientation.diagnose_inversion` now returns evidence, the status bar says `The item may be inverted at this destination. Review orientation before commit.`, and a `Turn it the right way up` control applies the turn when the reader agrees with it. That control refuses a socket that aims more than one descriptor row, because the correction would move every one of them. A move that needs an aim the item does not define creates an operation-owned child socket instead, named deterministically (`CDMW_<part>_<zone>[_role]_ChildSocket`, hashed when the readable form exceeds the 62 characters a `.paac` length prefix carries, numbered when a second operation sends the same row into the same zone) and routed only from this operation's rows. When the rotation is borrowed from another item that hangs there, only the rotation travels: the translation is the grip offset along the blade, and a two-hand sword's -0.470 would slide a shorter weapon off its own handle.
- **Animation scope is four presets with a per-context opt-in, and the confirmation button says what it will do.** `Everything` replaced standing, walking, running, turning, sitting, riding, combat and incidental clips with only a file count to judge it by. The scope is now `Placement only`, `Draw and stow only`, `Draw, stow, and stowed locomotion`, or `Full-body family replacement (advanced)`, which is never a default and is blocked until its confirmation is ticked; the eight context groups, mounted clips and the other playable character's clips are each opted into separately. Pair generation moved from a window callback to `carry.swappable_pairs(unit, entries, scope)`, so it reads the equipment unit rather than the current selection, matches the family on the exact parsed token (`sword` sits inside `longsword` and `dualsword`, so a substring match rewrote both grips from either one), and reports target and donor families apart, which is what makes a genuine one-handed target leak distinguishable from a valid one-handed donor. `replace_clip` refuses a path outside the operation's animation-target allowlist, a second enforcement layer behind the dialog's filtering. The move dialog is four pages (Equipment, Placement and linked parts, Animations, Review), shows vanilla, pending and proposed side by side so an earlier experiment cannot read as the game's default, prints raw socket names beside friendly labels and states that left and right are the character's, rebuilds every dependent value when the item changes, and labels its action `Move weapon`, `Move weapon and case, replace 14 animations`, `Replace 14 animations` or a disabled `No changes`. A placement no-op is no longer offered as a move.
- **A descriptor edit is written to the canonical file, not to the root-level alias.** `PlacementResolver.parts()` merged every loaded descriptor in iteration order, so `character/<name>.xml` won over `character/descriptors/characterdescription/<name>.xml` and became each row's `source_file`. The two are byte-identical in vanilla, so reads were unaffected, but `preview()` mirrors the canonical file onto the alias and not the reverse, so a routing edit recorded against the alias was never copied back and the package shipped the alias alone. A document whose alias source is also loaded is now skipped.
- **Modify Original has one `Advanced`, and it holds the texture-tuning controls with no box to tick.** Advanced was empty in Modify Original -- Material Authority is hidden for a clone -- and beside it sat a second section, `Advanced Texture Tuning`, whose controls only appeared once a checkbox inside it was ticked. The tuning group is Advanced's content now and is always shown. Whether a build actually writes material changes is decided by whether the reader tuned anything: an untouched session keeps the target's own materials, and moving any global tuning value or any per-part material adjustment (tint, colourise, brightness, contrast, saturation, gamma, glow, role) is what makes the build write a sidecar. The Output review reads the same predicate, so it says `replaces material bindings` exactly when the build will.
- **Import Mesh's Material Authority is one switch and one acknowledgement.** `Complete source-owned mesh/material swap` moves out of its own top-level `Materials` group into the Material Authority form, beside the runtime material profile it applies to, and it still opens armed for an external model. The `Unsafe Expert Controls` section is gone: its five routing checkboxes -- bind source textures, remove unused refs, create the missing base slot, neutralize inherited layers, reset inherited response -- are all forced on by the swap and off without it, which is what the operation classification already did with them, so they no longer ask the reader to decide five things the one switch already decided. `Allow unsafe material preflight export` stays, directly below the switch, because it means something on its own.
- **The `Options` section reads in working order.** The alignment controls (mode, scale to length, flip) come first and their group no longer repeats the word Options inside a section already called that; the Alignment Summary follows, then Import Notes when the import produced any, then Compatibility Details.
- **The Mesh Editor Builder's Setup tab is rearranged, and the Parts & Routing tab is gone.** Alignment mode, scale-to-length, and flip direction sat under Advanced and were found by nobody; they now live in the section at the top of Setup, which is renamed from `Summary` to `Options` because that is what it holds.
